{"parentUuid":"beb812f6-a01f-421d-a656-bfb5e16ae88e","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a89e2c94699dcff37","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-8","id":"msg_011Cd9rTR4VWSXCUA4aXeoBQ","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Here is the structured reconnaissance report. Everything below is what already exists in `/home/kali/sigil` — no proposed changes, just the reuse map.\n\n---\n\n# SIGIL substrate map for a gated OPERATOR AGENT\n\n## 1. Agent framework — `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/agents/base.py`\n\nThis is the file an `OperatorAgent` subclasses. Every effect flows through `_dispatch()`, which consults the Governor and writes to the spine. There is deliberately **no** external-effect code path in the base class.\n\n**`Tier(IntEnum)`** (lines 21-29) — mirror of WARDEN tiers, ordered so `<=` compares autonomy:\n```python\nA0 = 0   # observe / answer\nA1 = 1   # reversible internal act — the AUTO bar\nA2 = 2   # external-visible / semi-reversible — QUEUED\nA3 = 3   # destructive / financial / security — explicit, never auto\n```\n**`AUTO_BAR = Tier.A1`** (line 32) — \"A0/A1 auto-apply; A2/A3 queue for human approval\".\n\n**`Proposal`** dataclass (lines 35-42) — one thing an agent wants to do:\n```python\nkind: str            # spine record kind (event/finding/brief/interaction/draft/...)\npayload: dict\ntier: Tier = Tier.A1\nparent_id: Optional[int] = None\nsupersedes_id: Optional[int] = None\n```\n\n**`AgentResult`** dataclass (lines 45-50): `agent: str`, `applied: List[int]` (seqs written), `queued: List[dict]`, `notes: List[str]`.\n\n**`Agent`** base class (lines 53-100):\n- Class attrs: `name: str = \"AGENT\"`, `mandate: str = \"\"`, `ceiling: Tier = Tier.A1`.\n- `__init__(self, store: Optional[SpineStore] = None, *, governor=None)` — defaults a `SpineStore()` and lazily builds a `Governor(self.store)`.\n- `run(self) -> AgentResult` — `raise NotImplementedError` (subclass implements).\n- **`_dispatch(self, proposals: List[Proposal]) -> AgentResult`** (lines 71-100) — the enforcement core:\n  ```python\n  for p in proposals:\n      effective = max(p.tier, Tier.A0)\n      decision = self.governor.decide(agent=self.name, tier=effective,\n                                      ceiling=self.ceiling, scope=p.kind)\n      common = {**p.payload, \"agent\": self.name, \"tier\": effective.label(),\n                \"governor\": decision.reason}\n      if decision.outcome == Outcome.AUTO:\n          seq = self.store.append(kind=p.kind, source=AGENTS_SOURCE, actor=self.name,\n                                  payload={**common, \"decision\": \"auto\"}, ...)\n          res.applied.append(seq)\n      elif decision.outcome == Outcome.QUEUE:\n          seq = self.store.append(..., payload={**common, \"decision\": \"queued\",\n                                  \"status\": \"awaiting-approval\"}, ...)\n          res.queued.append({\"seq\": seq, \"kind\": p.kind, ...})\n      else:  # DENY — kill switch or budget; record the refusal, take no action\n          self.store.append(kind=\"refusal\", source=AGENTS_SOURCE, actor=self.name, ...)\n          res.notes.append(f\"DENIED {p.kind} ({effective.label()}): {decision.reason}\")\n  ```\n  Key facts for reuse: the **promotion scope is `p.kind`** (the real record kind written, not a self-asserted label). QUEUE still **writes** the record with `status: \"awaiting-approval\"` — nothing executes; the approval queue (§ below) later supersedes it. DENY writes a `refusal` record. `AGENTS_SOURCE = \"agent\"` (line 18) is the spine `source` field for all agent records, and the budget ledger keys on `source == \"agent\"`.\n\n**Doctrine constraint on any new agent module:** `sigil/governor/__init__.py` calls `assert_no_offense()` at import (`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/reuse/__init__.py:26`) — raises if any `framework.*` module is loaded. An OperatorAgent inherits this constraint transitively.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Governor / WARDEN gate — `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/governor/`\n\n### `core.py` — `Governor.decide()` (lines 66-92)\nSignature: `decide(self, *, agent: str, tier, ceiling, scope: Optional[str] = None) -> Decision`. `Outcome` enum = `AUTO | QUEUE | DENY` (lines 22-25); `Decision(outcome, reason)` frozen dataclass. The evaluation order is deliberately conservative and fail-closed:\n\n```python\nif tier <= Tier.A0:                      # A0 observe ALWAYS allowed — even under kill\n    return Decision(Outcome.AUTO, \"observe (A0)\")\nif self.kill.is_engaged():               # mesh halted; nothing above observe acts\n    return Decision(Outcome.DENY, \"kill switch engaged ...\")\nover, why = self.budget.over_budget(agent, self._day or _utc_day())\nif over:                                 # budget fail-closed\n    return Decision(Outcome.DENY, why)\nif tier <= AUTO_BAR and tier <= ceiling: # A0/A1 within ceiling → AUTO\n    return Decision(Outcome.AUTO, f\"within auto bar ({tier.label()})\")\nif tier == Tier.A2 and tier <= ceiling and self.promo.is_promoted(agent, scope):\n    return Decision(Outcome.AUTO, f\"A2 auto-approved by promotion ({agent}/{scope})\")\nreturn Decision(Outcome.QUEUE, f\"queued for approval ({tier.label()})\")  # A2 unpromoted, A3, or > ceiling\n```\n`Governor.__init__` (lines 55-64) composes `KillSwitch`, `PromotionPolicy`, `BudgetLedger`, and reads the persisted owner keypair/pubkey via `identity.owner_keypair()/owner_pubkey()`. `_load_caps()` reads `~/.sigil/budgets.json`.\n\n**How an operator's shell/file action gets tiered:** the Python Governor does **not** classify — it trusts the `tier` the Proposal declares (bounded by the agent `ceiling`). Tier classification of a *tool/command name* lives in the Rust kernel (§7). So an OperatorAgent would set `Proposal.tier` per action (A0/A1 for reads, A2/A3 for writes/destructive), and the Governor decides AUTO/QUEUE/DENY. The `ceiling` class attr is the structural cap.\n\n### `killswitch.py` (`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/governor/killswitch.py`)\n`SIGNAL = \"governor.killswitch\"`. `class KillSwitch(store, *, owner_key=None, trusted_pubkey=None)` with `engage(*, by, reason) -> int`, `release(...) -> int`, `is_engaged() -> bool`. **Asymmetric auth** (lines 38-49): ANY engage halts (fail-safe); a `release` un-halts **only if owner-signed and verifies** against the trusted pubkey. State is append-only spine `event` records `source=\"governor\", actor=\"WARDEN\"`. Read fresh per decision.\n\n### `budget.py` (`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/governor/budget.py`)\n`BudgetCaps(daily_actions, daily_interrupts)` (both default `None` = uncapped). `BudgetLedger.spent(agent, day_iso) -> (actions, interrupts)` derives spend by counting the agent's own spine records for the UTC day where `source==\"agent\"`, `actor==agent`, and `payload[\"decision\"] in (\"auto\",\"queued\")` (denials cost no budget; `kind==\"event\"` counts as an interrupt). `over_budget(agent, day_iso) -> (bool, str)` fail-closed at cap.\n\n### `promotion.py` (`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/governor/promotion.py`)\n`SIGNAL = \"governor.promotion\"`; `NO_PROMOTION_AGENTS = frozenset({\"ENVOY\"})`. `grant(agent, scope=\"*\")`, `revoke(...)`, `is_promoted(agent, scope) -> bool`. A grant/revoke is an owner-**signed** `event`; `is_promoted` ignores any grant that doesn't `verify_signed` against the trusted pubkey (lines 44-55), and honors a `(agent, \"*\")` wildcard grant. Scope = the record **kind**.\n\n### `identity.py` (`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/governor/identity.py`)\nTrust anchor = the same Ed25519 owner keypair the spine checkpoint uses: `KEYS_DIR/owner.priv|pub`. `owner_pubkey() -> Optional[str]` and `owner_keypair() -> Optional[KeyPair]` **read only** (fail-closed → None). `ensure_owner_keypair() -> KeyPair` generates+persists once (owner signing path only; `chmod 0o600` on priv).\n\n### `authn.py` (`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/governor/authn.py`)\nThe signing primitive shared by kill/promotion/approvals: `signed_payload(core: dict, owner_key) -> dict` (adds `sig`, `pubkey`); `verify_signed(payload, core_fields: Sequence[str], trusted_pubkey) -> bool` (fail-closed — no key/sig/tampered core → False). Built on `sigil.reuse.{canonical_json, sign, verify_one}`.\n\n---\n\n## 3. ARTIFICER — the sandboxing precedent — `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/agents/artificer.py`\n\n`class Artificer(Agent)`: `name=\"ARTIFICER\"`, **`ceiling = Tier.A2`**. This is the exact subprocess-safety pattern to reuse.\n\n**`_git(repo, *args, check=True) -> str`** (lines 26-30) — the entire git surface, list-argv (never `shell=True`), `-C repo` scoping:\n```python\nproc = subprocess.run([\"git\", \"-C\", repo, *args], capture_output=True, text=True)\nif check and proc.returncode != 0:\n    raise RuntimeError(f\"git {' '.join(args)} failed: {proc.stderr.strip()}\")\nreturn proc.stdout\n```\n\n**Worktree isolation** in `run(self, task, *, repo, test_cmd=None, coder=None, branch=None)` (lines 68-119):\n```python\n_git(repo, \"worktree\", \"prune\", check=False)\nwt = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=\"artificer-wt-\")\n_git(repo, \"worktree\", \"add\", \"-B\", branch, wt, base)\ntry:\n    summary = coder.code(task, wt)\n    changed = bool(_git(wt, \"status\", \"--porcelain\", check=False).strip())\n    diff = _git(wt, \"diff\", \"--stat\", check=False).strip()\n    ...\nfinally:\n    _git(repo, \"worktree\", \"remove\", \"--force\", wt, check=False)\n    shutil.rmtree(wt, ignore_errors=True)\n```\nThe coder works in a dedicated git **worktree** off `base`, so the real working tree is never touched and a failure leaves the repo untouched; the per-task branch survives for human review. Cleanup is in `finally`.\n\n**`test_cmd` execution** (line 99): `subprocess.run(test_cmd, cwd=wt, capture_output=True, text=True)` — argv list, run **in the worktree cwd**, return code gates the PR.\n\n**`_TRIVIAL_TEST` guard** (line 23): `frozenset({(), (\"true\",), (\":\",), (\"/bin/true\",), (\"/usr/bin/true\",)})`. `real_test = test_cmd is not None and tuple(test_cmd) not in _TRIVIAL_TEST` (line 90) — a PR may never claim \"tests passing\" on a no-op command.\n\n**Withhold / refuse semantics:**\n- No change → returns `AgentResult` with note, proposes nothing (lines 85-88).\n- Change but no real test → emits an A1 `finding` \"UNVERIFIED, PR withheld\" (lines 92-96).\n- Tests fail → A1 `finding` with `test_tail`, PR withheld (lines 100-106).\n- Tests pass → commit on branch, propose **A2 `pr`** (QUEUED) with `\"note\": \"...ARTIFICER does NOT push — a human reviews and pushes\"` (lines 108-115). `git push`/deploy are A3 and never invoked.\n\n**`ClaudeCoder`** (lines 43-60) — the \"shell to `claude -p`\" precedent:\n```python\ndef __init__(self, claude_bin: str = \"/home/kali/.local/bin/claude\", timeout: int = 600): ...\nproc = subprocess.run(\n    [self.claude_bin, \"-p\", prompt, \"--permission-mode\", \"acceptEdits\"],\n    cwd=workdir, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=self.timeout)\n```\nPluggable via `Coder` Protocol (line 38): `code(self, task: str, workdir: str) -> str`. `_slug(task)` (line 33) makes a stable unique branch name via `sha256_hex`.\n\n---\n\n## 4. BASTION — allowlist / refusal scope-gating — `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/agents/bastion.py`\n\n`class Bastion(Agent)`: `name=\"BASTION\"`, **`ceiling = Tier.A1`**. This is the scope-gating pattern to reuse for restricting an operator to allowlisted roots.\n\n**The allowlist** is built in `__init__` (line 141): `self._allow = {a.ref for a in self.inventory}` — \"Membership in the inventory IS the authorization to observe it.\" `Asset` (lines 28-35) = `name, kind, ref, meta`.\n\n**`_refuse(self, ref, reason) -> int`** (lines 147-153) — writes a `refusal` spine record with the requested ref, reason, and the doctrine string:\n```python\nreturn self.store.append(\n    kind=\"refusal\", source=\"agent\", actor=self.name,\n    payload={\"agent\": self.name, \"tier\": \"A0\", \"decision\": \"refused\",\n             \"requested\": ref, \"reason\": reason, \"doctrine\": \"...\"})\n```\n\n**`probe_target(self, ref, kind, *, now_iso=None) -> AgentResult`** (lines 155-163) — the guarded ad-hoc entry; the structural teeth:\n```python\nif ref not in self._allow:\n    self._refuse(ref, \"ad-hoc target is not in the own-infra allowlist\")\n    res = AgentResult(agent=self.name)\n    res.notes.append(f\"REFUSED {ref}: outside own-infra scope (logged, not scanned)\")\n    return res\nreturn self._dispatch(self._assess(Asset(name=ref, kind=kind, ref=ref), now_iso=now_iso))\n```\n**Defense in depth:** `_assess()` re-checks `asset.ref not in self._allow` again (lines 166-167) before ever scanning. Findings carry a verbatim `quote` (serve-the-quote grounding). Note the local-file read path in `_deps()` (line 207): `Path(asset.ref).read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"ignore\")` — an allowlisted path read.\n\n---\n\n## 5. SCHOLAR file reads / SSRF gate — `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/agents/sources.py`\n\n**`read_source(ref, *, timeout=20, max_chars=20000) -> str`** (lines 124-141) — the \"read this file/URL → text\" primitive; **never raises**, returns `\"\"` on failure/refusal:\n```python\nif ref.startswith((\"http://\", \"https://\")):\n    host = urlsplit(ref).hostname or \"\"\n    ip = _vetted_ip(host)\n    if ip is None:\n        return \"\"       # SSRF gate: non-public / unresolvable → no request leaves the machine\n    opener = urllib.request.build_opener(_PinnedHTTPHandler(ip), _PinnedHTTPSHandler(ip), _NoRedirect)\n    ...\nreturn Path(ref).read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"ignore\")[:max_chars]   # local path\n```\nLocal-path reading is the last line — `Path(ref).read_text(...)[:max_chars]`. Note: **the local-path branch is NOT itself path-gated** (no allowlist/traversal check) — it reads any path handed to it; scope-gating there is BASTION's `_allow` pattern's job, not `read_source`'s.\n\n**SSRF gate structure** (grounding \"read this URL\" as fact): `_vetted_ip(host) -> Optional[str]` (lines 31-57) resolves once, requires **every** resolved address be public unicast (rejects private/loopback/link-local/reserved/multicast/unspecified, unwraps IPv4-mapped IPv6), returns one IP to **pin**. `is_public_host(host) -> bool` (line 60). `_PinnedHTTPConnection` / `_PinnedHTTPSConnection` (lines 64-85) pin `create_connection` to the vetted IP while keeping SNI/cert validation on the original hostname; `_NoRedirect` refuses 3xx. `grounds_in_source(quote, source_text) -> bool` lives in `scholar.py` (verbatim + ≥2 salient tokens) — the epistemic check applied to what `read_source` returns.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Spine kinds + append — `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/spine/`\n\n### `models.py` — `KINDS` frozenset (lines 9-16)\n```python\nKINDS = frozenset({\n    \"message\", \"tool_call\", \"tool_result\", \"decision\", \"commitment\",\n    \"commit\", \"document\", \"brief\", \"refusal\", \"email\", \"session\",\n    \"entity\", \"contradiction\",        # ARCHIVIST consolidation\n    \"warden_checkpoint\",              # WARDEN cross-anchor\n    \"event\", \"finding\", \"interaction\", \"draft\",   # agent mesh: SENTINEL/BASTION/ENVOY\n    \"report\", \"pr\",                   # SCHOLAR reports, ARTIFICER PR proposals\n})\n```\nRelevant existing kinds an operator would touch: `refusal` (denials/out-of-scope), `event` (kill/promotion/approval signals + interrupts), `finding` (observations), `tool_call` / `tool_result` (there is already a pair for tool invocations). An operator that runs shell/file ops would most naturally map to `tool_call`/`tool_result` for the action record, `refusal` for denials, and `pr`-like proposal kinds for queued writes — **no operator-specific kind exists yet** (e.g. no `operation`/`fs_write`/`command`). Note `KINDS` is defined but **not enforced** in `append()` — `store.append` does not validate `kind` against `KINDS`, so a new kind works without editing the frozenset (though the frozenset is the documented registry).\n\n`SpineRecord` (lines 24-49) is the frozen record with chain fields `cert_digest`/`prev_hash`/`entry_hash`; `.text()` extracts human text from payload keys `(\"text\",\"content\",\"message\",\"summary\",\"body\",\"title\")` for embedding/display.\n\n### `store.py` — `SpineStore.append()` (lines 49-67)\n```python\ndef append(self, *, kind: str, source: str, actor: str, payload: dict[str, Any],\n           parent_id: int | None = None, supersedes_id: int | None = None,\n           ts: str | None = None) -> int:\n    content = {\"scope\": SCOPE, \"kind\": kind, \"source\": source, \"actor\": actor,\n               \"payload\": payload, \"parent_id\": parent_id, \"supersedes_id\": supersedes_id}\n    cert_digest = digest_payload(content)            # wallclock-free → replay-stable\n    entry = append_entry([self._last], cert_digest) if self._last else build_chain([cert_digest])[0]\n    ...append JSONL line... ; return entry.seq\n```\nAppend-only, hash-chained (reuses CRUCIBLE chain from `sigil.reuse`). Reads: `iter_records(*, since_seq=-1)`, `get(seq)`, `next_seq`, `count()`. Integrity: `verify() -> (bool, str)` (two-layer: payload-binding + chain). `SCOPE` comes from `config.py` (`SIGIL_SCOPE`, default `\"sigil\"`).\n\n**Approval queue** (`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/agents/approvals.py`) is how a QUEUED operator write would get owner sign-off: `SIGNAL=\"governor.approval\"`, `_QUEUED_STATUS=\"awaiting-approval\"`. `pending(store, trusted_pubkey_b64) -> List` returns queued items with no **verified** owner approval. `ApprovalQueue.approve(seq, ...)/deny(seq, ...)` require the signing key to **be** the trusted owner key (RP-APPROVAL-2), sign `(target, decision, approver)`, and append an `event` with `supersedes_id=seq`.\n\n---\n\n## 7. All other subprocess/shell + the tier classifier\n\n### The command-classification / danger-detection logic lives in the Rust kernel (`/home/kali/sigil/kernel/src/tiers.rs`)\nThis is the closest thing to a \"danger detector\" for shell/tool names — **token-based, fail-closed, danger-first**. `pub fn classify(tool: &str) -> Tier` (lines 122-142):\n```rust\nif has(A3_TOKENS) { return Tier::A3; }\nif has(A2_TOKENS) { return Tier::A2; }\nif has(A1_TOKENS) { return Tier::A1; }\nif A0_TOOLS.contains(&full.as_str()) || has(A0_VERBS) { return Tier::A0; }\nTier::A3   // not positively classified → fail-closed to the most-gated tier\n```\n`tokens()` (lines 113-118) splits on `. _ - /` + whitespace into **whole tokens** (\"overwrite\" ≠ \"write\", \"forget\" ≠ \"get\"). Token sets (lines 64-110):\n- **A3_TOKENS**: destructive verbs (`push, deploy, delete, destroy, drop, remove, purge, wipe, truncate, overwrite, erase, format, kill, shutdown, reboot, reset, disable, enable, override, force, sudo, exec, eval, chmod, chown, patch, install, uninstall`), crypto/restore (`encrypt, decrypt, restore, revert, rollback, recover`), financial, and **dangerous targets regardless of verb** (`secret(s), credential(s), token(s), key(s), iam, policy, firewall, acl, role, grant, revoke, rotate, escalate, infra, prod, production, env, master, root, admin, vault, keyring, keychain, keystore, hsm`).\n- **A2_TOKENS**: `send, email, smtp, publish, post, message, outbound, webhook, sms, notify, share, upload, invite, calendar, tweet, dm, export, dump, download, sync`.\n- **A1_TOKENS**: `write, note, brief, report, draft, alert, consolidate, commit, branch, annotate, tag, label`.\n- **A0_VERBS** (positive allowlist, verbs only): `read, search, query, get, list, status, recall, observe, answer, view, show, find, frame, peek, describe, inspect, lookup`; plus exact-name **A0_TOOLS** for the read-only memory tools.\n\n`pub fn gate(tier) -> Decision` (lines 144-150): A0/A1 → `Auto`, A2 → `Queued`, A3 → `ExplicitRequired`. Note this maps the same A0-A3 → auto/queue/explicit as the Python Governor, but the **classifier itself is Rust-only** — there is no Python port. Directly relevant to an operator: `exec`, `eval`, `sudo`, `chmod`, `chown`, `install`, `rm`-shaped verbs (`delete/remove/wipe`) all classify **A3**; and unknown tool names fail closed to A3.\n\n`registry.rs` (`/home/kali/sigil/kernel/src/registry.rs`) — `Registry::tier(tool)` returns `max(pin, inferred)`: pins from `~/.sigil/warden/tools.json` are **RAISE-ONLY** (an attacker-writable file can only make a tool *more* gated, never downgrade `git.push`).\n\n`warden.rs` (`/home/kali/sigil/kernel/src/warden.rs`) — `Warden::decide(tool) -> Verdict{tier, decision}` (pure, no side effects) and `Warden::record(...)` appends a signed action-log record. \"WARDEN never executes tools itself; it decides and it records.\" `main.rs` exposes `ask`/`do`/`audit`/`verify`/`checkpoint`/`pubkey` subcommands; `cmd_do` (lines 139-150) classifies + gates a named tool but has **no real executors** (\"those are the Phase-3 agents\").\n\n### The Python↔kernel bridge that already exists\n`/home/kali/sigil/sigil/voice/dispatch.py` — `KernelDispatch.send(text)` shells the kernel binary as an argv list and parses status lines:\n```python\nproc = subprocess.run([self.kernel_bin, \"ask\", text], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=self.timeout)\n```\n`_default_bin()` looks for `kernel/target/release/sigil-kernel` then `debug`. There is currently **no** Python call to a kernel `classify`/`do` for tiering an agent action — the Rust classifier is reachable only via the `sigil-kernel` CLI (`do <tool>`), not imported into Python.\n\n### Every other subprocess/shell call in the Python tree (all argv-list, none `shell=True`)\n- `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/agents/artificer.py` — git + `claude -p` + `test_cmd` (§3).\n- `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/agents/scholar.py:57` — `ClaudeSynthesizer`: `subprocess.run([self.claude_bin, \"-p\", prompt, \"--model\", self.model], ...)`; pluggable `Synthesizer` Protocol; wrapped in try/except returning `[]`.\n- `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/consolidate/extract.py:132` — `subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=self.timeout)` (a third `claude -p`-style shell for entity extraction).\n- `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/ingest/git.py:40` and `ingest/hooks.py:21` — read-only `git` reads for ingestion.\n- `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/perception/capture.py:49,65,80` — `tesseract` / screenshot (`scrot` etc.) / `ffmpeg` captures, each guarded by `shutil.which(...)`, `timeout`, and `try/except (SubprocessError, OSError)`.\n- `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/voice/dispatch.py:33` — kernel bridge (above).\n- `/home/kali/sigil/sigil/agents/sentinel.py:45` — `shutil.disk_usage` (read).\n\n**Consistent safety idiom across all of them:** argv lists (never `shell=True` anywhere in the repo), `capture_output=True, text=True`, a `timeout`, and `except (subprocess.SubprocessError, OSError)` returning a benign empty/error value rather than raising.\n\n---\n\n## Concrete reuse points for an OperatorAgent (map only, no changes proposed)\n\n- **Subclass** `Agent` (`base.py`), set a `ceiling` (ARTIFICER's `A2` is the precedent for \"propose writes, queue for approval\"; BASTION's `A1` for observe-only reads), emit `Proposal`s, and let `_dispatch()` route every action through `Governor.decide()` and onto the spine. No new gate code is needed — the AUTO/QUEUE/DENY machinery already exists.\n- **Reads → A0/A1** auto; **writes/destructive → A2/A3** queue. This is the pattern that would restrict an operator to allowlisted folders — noting `read_source`'s local-path branch is itself *unguarded*, so the allowlist must wrap it.\n- **Ground \"read this file\" facts** with `read_source()` (local path) + SCHOLAR's serve-the-quote (`grounds_in_source`).\n- **Persist** via `store.append(kind=..., source=\"agent\", actor=\"OPERATOR\", payload=..., parent_id=..., supersedes_id=...)`.
